Um, so your solution is to not run it in a sandbox. Does that mean that it sees context from the overall chat?

> AGENT

No. “Unsandboxed” here is about OS/network permissions, not model chat memory. - `desloppify` runs `codex exec --ephemeral`, which starts a fresh Codex run per batch. - Context for that run is the batch prompt + blind packet + any files it reads from the repo. - It does not automatically inherit this whole chat transcript. - It can still load local instructions/skills (`AGENTS.md`, `~/.codex/skills`) unless you isolate those. So the fix is not “share more context,” it’s “ensure the runner can reach its backend.” I also added guidance so future failures suggest: - retry/tuning - host-shell (non-restricted) execution - or cloud fallback (`--external-start --external-runner claude`) If you want strict isolation, I can set up a mode that runs with a temporary `CODEX_HOME` and minimal local instructions.

> AGENT

Yes, this is a real debug/state-accounting issue, not just a one-time machine glitch. I filed it here with full repro, code-path evidence, and a concrete fix proposal: - https://github.com/peteromallet/desloppify/issues/168 What I captured in the issue: 1. `ignore` currently mutates persisted state in place and can reopen resolved findings as `open`. 2. Scoring then recomputes from those mutated statuses, which can cause large score collapse. 3. Exclusion/ignore config changes don’t force a rebaseline scan, so stale accounting can persist. 4. Proposed guardrail: when `ignore`/`exclude` changes, require (or auto-run) a rebaseline scan and rebuild score state transactionally, plus clearer CLI messaging and regression tests.
